# BGE Q3 and Q4 2017 Semi-Annual EmPOWER Maryland Report (Case No. 9154)

**Source**: ml218796_bge_2017_q3q4_empower.pdf
**Pages**: 127 total (partial extract; pages 84-88 and 94-116 are not transcribed)
**Date**: January 31, 2018 (date of the cover letter)
**Author(s)**: Kimberly A. Curry, Assistant General Counsel, BGE Legal Department (cover letter, to David J. Collins, Executive Secretary, Maryland Public Service Commission)

_Extractor note (not source text): the filing is BGE's semi-annual EmPOWER Maryland report for the third and fourth quarters (July 1 through December 31, 2017) in Case No. 9154, with full-year 2017, 2015-2017 cycle-to-date and program-to-date results. The PDF prints no Maillog (ML) number; ML 218796 comes from the request that produced this extract. Running headers (the cover letter's "David J. Collins, Executive Secretary / January 31, 2018 / Page N") and footers ("BGE Q3/Q4 2017 EmPOWER Maryland Portfolio Reporting Page N") are left out; the page marker carries the printed page number. Printed page numbers: cover letter pages 1-13 (page 1 prints no number); the report proper restarts at 2 on PDF p. 15 (PDF p. 14 is an unnumbered title page); the Appendix A and B spreadsheet pages (PDF pp. 69-82, 84-88) and Attachment 1 (PDF pp. 94-116) print no page number._

_Extractor note (not source text): this is a partial extract. Other narrative is transcribed as printed; charts, images and tables outside the heat pump scope (lighting, appliance collection, marketing) are described or summarized in bracketed notes where they occur. RE: BGE’s Semi-Annual Report for Third and Fourth Quarters – July 1 through
December 31, 2017 in Case No. 9154
Dear Mr. Collins:

Enclosed for filing is the EmPOWER Maryland report for the two quarters ended December 31,
2017, full year 2017 results, cycle-to-date results and program-to-date as of December 31, 2017.

## Executive Summary

### Program Participation and Impact Highlights - 2017:

The second half and full year 2017 demonstrated the continuing strength of BGE’s Smart Energy
Savers Program®, the umbrella for programs supporting EmPOWER Maryland. Based on a
solid foundation of the traditional residential and commercial energy efficiency and
PeakRewards℠ programs and augmented by BGE’s more recent innovative Smart Energy
Rewards®, Smart Energy Manager®, and Conservation Voltage Reduction programs, BGE
delivered significant energy savings and demand reductions to its customers’ homes and
businesses. The second half of 2017 also saw BGE beginning deployment of new, cutting edge
Smart Thermostats across multiple EmPOWER platforms to equip BGE’s customers with yet
another tool for saving energy and reducing their energy costs.

BGE’s traditional Residential and Commercial Energy Efficiency programs produced nearly
215,000 MWh annualized gross wholesale savings for the third and fourth quarters compared to
the target of 177,000 MWh approved by the Commission (including Commission approved
changes). Full year savings were over 370,000 MWh compared to a target of 351,000 MWh for
the year. Residential program savings were 106,000 MWh for the second half and nearly
176,000 MWh for the full year representing 127% and 112% of targets, respectively.

Commercial programs continued the trend of representing a greater portion of energy savings

[PDF p. 2 | printed p. 2]

representing 55% of combined traditional energy efficiency savings during 2017. For the second
half of 2017, savings from Commercial programs were 109,000 MWh and 195,000 MWh for the
full year representing 117% and 101% of the 2017 targets, respectively. Combined, Residential
and Commercial savings for the full year were over 105% of the 2017 target.

Savings from Smart Energy Manager®, BGE’s residential behavioral energy efficiency program,
contributed energy savings of over 145,000 MWh (160% of target) for the year and the CVR or
Conservation Voltage Reduction program savings added nearly 138,000 MWh (135% of target).

Total energy savings from all programs for 2017, as illustrated below, were over 668,000 MWh.

This equates to 2.05% of 2013 weather normalized gross wholesale retail sales.

| Utilty [sic] | 2017 Reported Savings[^l1] (MWh) | 2013 Energy Sales[^l2] (MWh) | Percent of Sales | Interim 2017 Goal |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| BGE | 668,340 | 32,602,459 | 2.05% | 1.94% |

As of December 31, 2017, the potential and installed capacity demand reduction from all BGE
programs was 1,294 MW. Of this total, Energy Efficiency and PeakRewards℠ programs
accounted for 511 MW and 357 MW, respectively and the Smart Energy Rewards®, BGE’s peak
time rebate program, contributed 330 MW with an additional 97 MW from other programs.

In addition to reducing electric usage, BGE’s EmPOWER programs helped customers reduce
natural gas usage. During 2017, BGE’s traditional residential and commercial energy efficiency
programs reduced gas usage by approximately 1.1 million therms. Smart Energy Manager®,
BGE’s residential behavioral energy efficiency program, reduced gas usage by approximately
2.6 million therms.

Participation in the traditional energy efficiency programs continued to grow, with nearly
126,000 participants in the second half of 2017 and 213,000 for the full year 2017. For the full
three year cycle participation was 677,000 and over 1,997,000 Program-to-Date. (Note that a
customer generally is counted as a unique participant every time they get an incentive or
participate in a particular program. See Appendix J: Notes and Assumptions for how lighting
is counted). More than 405,000 unique residential customers participated in BGE’s energy
efficiency Program-to-Date, excluding the Lighting Program where unique customer information
cannot be collected. As of year-end 2017, PeakRewards℠ had a total of more than 345,000 A/C
and over 22,000 Water Heater customers participating with a total of over 367,000 active devices
in both programs.

[^l1]: 2017 Reported Savings are at the gross wholesale level.

[^l2]: 2013 Energy Sales are reported at gross wholesale levels and weather-normalized.

[PDF p. 3 | printed p. 3]

## Residential Energy Efficiency

BGE’s Residential program achieved 124% of its overall portfolio gross wholesale MWh
forecast for the second half of 2017.

- For the full year, BGE achieved just under 176,000 gross wholesale MWh achieving
  112% of its forecasted target.
  - The lighting markdown program achieved over 131,000 MWh which reflects over
    127,000 participants. Over 3 million energy efficient bulbs were sold in 2017.
  - The Quick Home Energy Checkup program achieved nearly 15,000 MWh of
    energy savings achieving 115% of the forecast. Over 36,000 customers
    participated in this program with over 462,000 energy efficient measures being
    installed during the year.
  - The residential new construction program continued its success achieving 135%
    of its forecasted MWh reflecting over 3,200 energy efficient homes constructed.
  - The Recycling Program achieved 8,700 MWh which reflects just under 90% of
    the forecasted energy savings for 2017.

## Commercial and Industrial (C&I) Energy Efficiency

The C&I portfolio had an outstanding year with most of its major C&I programs exceeding their
forecast.

- During the second half of 2017, the C&I portfolio yielded 109,000 MWh compared to its
  forecast of 93,000 MWh achieving 117% of its second half forecast.
- For the full year, the C&I portfolio delivered 194,000 MWh in energy savings and
  achieving 101% of its 2017 forecast.
  - The Energy Solutions for Business (ESfB) Program continued to be a strong
    contributor towards BGE’s C&I portfolio with over 92,000 MWh or 119% of its
    annual energy savings forecast.
  - BGE had two CHP projects which went online and started generating savings in

2017. The 2 MW projects at Baltimore Washington Medical Center and the
      University of Maryland St. Joseph’s Medical Center provided over 25,000 MWh.
      - BGE’s Commercial Midstream Lighting (Instant Lighting Discounts Program)
        contributed over 45,000 MWh and achieving 158% of its 2017 forecast.
      - The Building Tune-up and Building Operators Training programs greatly
        accelerated in the second half of the year to deliver over 13,000 MWh or 218% of
        the annual energy saving forecast.

[PDF p. 4 | printed p. 4]

## PeakRewards℠ Program

BGE PeakRewards℠, BGE’s residential demand response program, continued to operate
smoothly with more than 312,000 Air Conditioning and 22,000 Water Heater customers
receiving credits.

Coincident with the planning for the 2018 – 2020 three-year cycle, BGE revised the per device
point estimate for PeakRewards℠ devices using data available through AMI meters. This
resulted in an overall reduction in the estimated demand reduction available from these devices
of approximately 40 MW.

## BGE Smart Energy Rewards®

BGE Smart Energy Rewards®, BGE’s residential behavioral peak time rebate program, included
over one million customers with smart meters for the summer 2017 season. The program is
designed to encourage customers to use less electricity during summer peak demand periods
called Energy Savings Days (ESDs). On two separate occasions throughout the summer, BGE
notified customers by email, text or phone call of an upcoming ESD. Customers earned $1.25 in
bill credits for every kWh reduced, compared to their baseline.

### BGE Smart Energy Manager® (SEM):

Smart Energy Manager® (SEM), BGE’s residential behavioral energy efficiency program, has
over 920,000 customers enrolled and over 14.8 million Home Energy Reports have been sent to
customers since program inception.

### Conservation Voltage Reduction (CVR):

Through the end of 2017 BGE has deployed CVR technology on 494 circuits. Savings from the
CVR program deployments amounted to over 137,000 MWh for 2017. These savings are based
on circuits deployed through 2017 based on evaluated reductions through the end of 2016. Since
the beginning of CVR deployment, this program has provided reductions to BGE residential,
commercial and industrial customers of nearly 331,000 MWh.

### Industry Recognitions:

BGE’s programs were also widely recognized for their performance during 2017, including
receipt of the following industry recognitions:

- BGE was ranked fourth among 51 U.S. electric utilities in ACEEE’s first “Utility Energy
  Efficiency Scorecard”
- In J. D. Power’s 2017 Utility Products and Services Study, BGE was ranked second,
  among 26 utilities, for both residential and business customer awareness of their program
  offerings and first and third in customer participation for residential and business
  respectively

[PDF p. 5 | printed p. 5]

- BGE has attained ENERGY STAR’s Sustained Excellence Partner of the Year award for
  the past seven years
- BGE received the ENERGY STAR Certified Homes Market Leader Award
- BGE was recognized as Analytics Project of the Year at the May 2017 CS Week
  conference
- BGE received the 2017 Oracle Sustainability Award for the environmental impact of the
  behavioral programs

## Program Cost Breakdown

In 2017, nearly $65 million was spent on energy efficiency incentives, and more than $211
million Cycle-to-Date. All incentive spending was below the Commission approved budget
including Commission approved additions. In addition to these incentives received, BGE
customers will benefit with over $330 million in lifecycle premise savings from their
participation in 2017 and more than $1.8 billion Cycle-to-Date. Program-to-Date, over $505
million has been spent on energy efficiency incentives and customers will see a benefit of nearly
$4 billion over the expected useful life of the energy saving measures installed.

For the Year, Cycle and Program-to-Date all other cost categories were below the Commission
approved budget including Commission approved additions.

Energy Efficiency total expenditures for the second half of 2017 were 87% of the Commission-
approved filed budget. This was due primarily to lower incentive costs in both Residential and
Commercial programs and all other costs also lower than budgeted. For the full year spending
was approximately 79% of budgeted. The following chart depicts the breakdown by cost
category for the second half of the year:

[DIAGRAM DESCRIPTION: Pie chart titled "Q3/Q4 2017 EE Costs by Category". Percent of cost by category: Utility Administration 2%, Outside Services 15%, Marketing 5%, Evaluation 3%, Customer Incentives 75%.]
[→ See original PDF page 5 for visual rendering]

Note – Does not include costs associated with implementation of the Limited Income Energy
Efficiency Program (LIEEP) managed by the Maryland Department of Housing & Community
Development.

[PDF p. 6 | printed p. 6]

PeakRewards℠ total spending was approximately 90% of the second half as-filed budget due
largely to lower capital costs related to the number of devices installed. For the full year,
spending was approximately 91% of budgeted. The following chart depicts the breakdown by
cost category:

[DIAGRAM DESCRIPTION: Pie chart titled "Q3/4 2017 PeakRewards Costs by Category". Percent of cost by category: O&M 6%, Capital 18%, Marketing Costs 6%, Customer Bonus 1%, Customer Incentives 69%.]
[→ See original PDF page 6 for visual rendering]

Over $21 million was returned to customers as bill credits during peak months or as the first year
of installation signing bonus for new devices during 2017.

### Program Participation and Impact Highlights – 2015 – 2017 Cycle:

With 2017, came the final year of the 2015 – 2017 EmPOWER Maryland cycle. For the three
year cycle, BGE’s traditional residential and commercial energy efficiency programs produced
nearly 1.473,000 [sic] MWh annualized gross wholesale savings for the current cycle three years
compared to the target of 1.426,000 [sic] MWh (103% of target) approved by the Commission
(including Commission approved changes). The traditional energy efficiency Residential
programs achieved 109% of target while Commercial programs achieved 91% of target.

In the final year of the 2015 – 2017 cycle, per capita usage for BGE’s service territory was
approximately 11.1 MWh, an approximate 17.1% reduction from the 2007 baseline usage
specified in the original EmPOWER goals, an improvement over the 10% reduction by 2015
mandated in the original EmPOWER act and achieved by BGE in 2015. See chart below:

[PDF p. 7 | printed p. 7]

[DIAGRAM DESCRIPTION: Bar chart titled "2017 Energy Use vs. 2015 EmPOWER Goal" (vertical axis: MWh, 28,000,000 to 36,000,000). Four bars, each labelled with total MWh above the bar and per-capita MWh inside the bar: 2007 Baseline Usage 35,109,765 MWh (13.41); Energy Use Goal 2015 MWh 33,164,879 (12.07); Energy Use 2015 32,642,002 (11.88); Energy Use 2017 30,896,776 (11.11). Red annotation in the chart: "17.2% Per Capita Use Reduction from 2007 Baseline Using Est. 12/17 Population and 12 Month Energy Use".]
[→ See original PDF page 7 for visual rendering]

_[source discrepancy: the text on page 6 gives the per capita reduction from the 2007 baseline as "approximate 17.1%"; the chart on this page is annotated 17.2%.]_

For the year 2017, an interim target was established to replace the 2015 goal which had expired.

BGE’s target was savings of 1.94% of 2013 sales. BGE exceeded this target achieving savings
2.05% of 2013 sales. The middle year of the cycle had no specified target but BGE also
achieved savings of 2.05% of the 2013 baseline sales for that year. The average of the 2016 and
2017 savings compared to the 2013 baseline sales was 2.05%.

In addition to the electric savings for the cycle, BGE’s EmPOWER programs helped its
customers reduce natural gas usage by more than 10,000,000 therms. During the 2015 - 2017
cycle, BGE’s traditional Residential and Commercial Energy Efficiency programs helped
customers conserve natural gas with savings of approximately 3.4 million therms. Smart Energy
Manager®, BGE’s residential behavioral energy efficiency program, helped BGE customers
conserve an aggregate of approximately 7.1 million therms.

### Cycle Costs:

Over the three year cycle, approximately 77% of the expenditures by BGE on EmPOWER
programs were returned to BGE’s customers in the form of incentives, bill credits, bonuses,
rebates and measures installed in their homes and businesses. In aggregate, spend for the cycle
was approximately 74% of the approved 2015 – 2017 budget including Commission approved
additions.

### Program Participation and Impact Highlights – Program-to-Date:

In total, program-to-date BGE programs have produced annualized energy savings of 3,582,000
MWh. BGE’s traditional Residential and Commercial Energy Efficiency programs total
annualized energy savings is over 3,226,000 MWh since 2008. The Smart Energy Manager®
program has saved customers 511,000 MWh in aggregate since inception in 2013. The
Conservation Voltage Reduction (CVR) program has saved 330,000 MWh in aggregate since the
beginning of deployment in 2014. All other EmPOWER programs combined provided
annualized energy savings of nearly 80,000 MWh to date.

[DIAGRAM DESCRIPTION: Stacked column chart titled "BGE Energy Efficiency Savings by Program" (vertical axis: Gross Wholesale MWh, 0 to 800,000; horizontal axis: 2008 through 2017). Each column is stacked by Residential EE, Commercial EE, Residential Behaviorial [sic], CVR and Other; the chart prints no data labels. Visually, columns start near 75,000 MWh in 2008, are mostly Residential EE in 2008-2009, add Commercial EE from 2009, add Residential Behaviorial from 2013 and CVR from 2014, and reach roughly 650,000-670,000 MWh in 2016 and 2017.]
[→ See original PDF page 9 for visual rendering]

Program-to-date, BGE’s traditional Residential and Commercial Energy Efficiency programs
have contributed savings of approximately 9.7 million therms. Since inception, Smart Energy
Manager®, BGE’s residential behavioral energy efficiency program helped BGE customers
conserve an aggregate of approximately 8.2 million therms saved.

BGE’s customers can also breathe a little easier knowing that, combined, the electric and gas
reductions from participating in BGE’s EmPOWER programs equate to removing over 28
million metric tons of greenhouse gases from the environment.

[PDF p. 10 | printed p. 10]

In concert with the electric and gas savings, BGE’s Smart Energy Savers Program® provides
tangible economic benefits to BGE customers. From 2008 through the end of 2017, BGE’s
programs have created nearly $5 billion in such benefits for our customers:

| Benefit (2008 through the end of 2017) | Amount |

| Direct rebates paid to customers for energy efficiency measures | $505,000,000 |
| Lifecycle savings over the useful life of energy efficiency measures | $4,200,000,000 |
| PeakRewards℠ customer credits and signing bonuses paid to customers | $227,000,000 |
| SER program credits paid to customers | $46,000,000 |

The Summary Tables accompanying this report include additional Commission approved
incentives to programs and transfers between programs with any related corresponding savings
approved reflecting the success of BGE’s programs in driving customer participation.

## Enhancements

- Residential Energy Efficiency program enhancements
  - The HPwES Program instituted a significant overhaul in the incentive structure
    for the second half of 2017. Moving from a percentage of cost based incentive
    structure to a performance based incentive structure allowed for more
    comprehensive work-scopes by removing the project SIR requirement. As a
    result of performance based incentives, customer contract costs and measures
    increased by 131% and 123%, respectively.
  - BGE began offering Appliance Recycling customers the opportunity to enroll in
    the BGE QHEC program the same day as their unit pick up date. BGE hopes to
    target customers who are at home during the day for their appliance collection,
    adding greater customer convenience to both programs.
- Commercial & Industrial Energy Efficiency program enhancements
  - While no major enhancements were made in 2017, several new enhancements for
    the 2018 -2020 program cycle will be implemented in 2018. Please refer to
    BGE’s 2018 – 2020 EmPOWER Maryland filing for these program changes.
- Demand Response program enhancements
  - BGE launched a PeakRewards℠ smart thermostat program in the third quarter of

2017. The PeakRewards℠ smart thermostat program will address BGE
      customers’ growing interest in technology and provide additional demand
      response and energy efficiency benefits.

- Behavioral program enhancements
  - BGE’s Smart Energy Manager program launched a new Net Metering online widget
    which allows Net Metering customers to view their energy credit balances from
    month to month.
  - The Smart Energy Manager Program launched a dual fuel version of the Weekly
    Usage Report which provides dual fuel (gas and electric) customers a comparison of
    both their gas and electric usage week over week.

[PDF p. 11 | printed p. 11]

### BGE’s Smart Thermostat Programs:

Per Order No. 88514 dated December 22, 2017, the information below responds to ordering
paragraph (16); “That each Utility offering smart thermostats through multiple programs are
directed to describe the interaction of the measures with the Utility’s demand response portfolio
as part of the forthcoming January 31, 2018 reports.”

During 2017, BGE began offering new, innovative smart thermostats across multiple
EmPOWER programs as another tool to help its customers achieve additional energy savings.

The programs included PeakRewards℠ (direct install of a smart thermostat as part of
participation), Quick Home Energy Check-up (cross-selling the PeakRewards℠ program and
simultaneous customer delivery of both programs) and the Residential New Construction
Program (installation of a smart thermostat in addition to the ENERGY STAR energy efficiency
requirements).

Beginning in 2018 and as approved by the Commission through Order No. 88514, BGE will
expand our 2017 offers to include multiple smart thermostat program options for our customers.

There are four residential options and one small business option.

For residential customers electing to participate in PeakRewards℠, BGE offers an installed smart
thermostat at no additional cost. For customers who are not participating in PeakRewards℠,
beginning in 2018, BGE is providing financial incentives to residential customers who purchase
eligible smart thermostats. Whether obtained as part of BGE’s PeakRewards℠ program or
purchased as part of the Smart Thermostat Program, residential customers can recognize
additional energy savings by replacing a manually operated or conventional programmable
thermostat. In addition, all qualified thermostats are eligible to enroll in the optimization tract,
providing customer’s additional energy savings.

In 2018 BGE’s Small Business Energy Solutions Program added smart thermostats as an eligible
measure. Smart thermostats capabilities make them ideal for small business energy efficiency
applications.

The Table below details BGE’s various Thermostat Programs, associated incentives and bill
credits where applicable, how the thermostat can be obtained and where customers can obtain
additional information.

BGE will continue to refine and enhance the evaluation and reporting for Smart Thermostats as it
increases participation and measures.

[PDF p. 12 | printed p. 12]

## Certification

Pursuant to Maryland Public Utilities Article § 7-211(h)(6), BGE hereby certifies that it does not subsidize the operations of an affiliate providing heating, ventilation, air conditioning, or refrigeration services through any existing contract or obligation in connection with the electric company’s efficiency program or service. BGE’s affiliate participates in its energy efficiency program under the same terms and conditions applicable to all contractors. BGE does not promote, or extend any preference to its affiliate.

## Semi-Annual Report for Third and Fourth Quarters 2017

## Program Specifics

## Programs in Ramp-Up Phase

- No programs were in ramp-up phase.

## Implemented Residential and Commercial Programs

## Residential Lighting Program

### Program Highlights:

In Q3/Q4 2017, the Lighting Program achieved 132% of the net wholesale savings goal, and
119% of the full year savings goal. BGE was able to achieve more savings with less incentive
spend in 2017 by focusing on SKUs that can achieve higher savings per bulb, as well as
multipacks, which have lower prices per bulb.

- In an effort to boost sales in Q3/Q4 and to take advantage of the fall lighting season, BGE
  scheduled multiple limited time promotions. The promotions were in eight different
  retailers, and included limited-time increased incentives on select SKUs. Multiple
  retailers provided off-shelf placement for participating SKUs, including wing stacks,
  endcaps and pallets, for the duration of the promotion.
- In Q3/Q4, BGE placed an increased focus on engaging independent hardware retailers by
  distributing a letter directed at independent hardware store owners and managers. The
  letter explained the lighting program, processes, and how to participate. BGE also held
  special, limited time promotions with independent hardware retailers. These efforts
  helped increase sales through the program at independent hardware stores by 1500% from
  Q1/Q2.
- During the second-half of 2017, a Philips Hue Connected bulb SKU was added at The
  Home Depot stores. The year ended with 1,000 connected bulb sales.
- In Q4, BGE ran their first limited time online lighting promotion and emailed over
  500,000 customers and BGE employees a limited time offer of 10 LEDs and one LED
  desk lamp for $10. As a bonus, customers also had the option to purchase 6 bulged
  reflector (BR) bulbs for $10. The employee promotion and the customer promotion each
  ran for one week. Over 6,800 orders were placed for over 123,000 bulbs.
- In Q4 2017, BGE’s Lighting Food Bank program distributed 18,436 ENERGY STAR
  LED bulbs to limited income BGE residential customers through partnerships with 15
  local food pantries. In July 2017, BGE participated in two onsite distribution events with
  the Salvation Army – Baltimore, distributing the LED bulbs and lighting educational
  flyers to limited income customers. The BGE staff on hand spoke with customers about
  the benefits of LED lighting in the home.
- In 2017, BGE’s Lighting Food Bank program distributed a total of 57,220 ENERGY
  STAR LED bulbs to limited income BGE residential customers who may not have the
  means to shop traditional retail for energy efficient lighting.

[PDF p. 18 | printed p. 5]

- In Q3/Q4 2017, BGE’s Lighting Pop Up Retail Program hosted 15 Pop Up Retail events,
  offering specially incentivized ENERGY STAR LED bulbs to residential customers
  through local businesses and community events. In 2017, BGE hosted a total of 24 Pop
  Up Retail events.

Lighting units sold in Q3 and Q4 by retail channel are shown below.

- A limited time promotion was launched on November 27, 2017 and ran through December

Customers were also able to add a bonus offer of 10 BR30 bulbs in warm or cool light for
$10 at check out. The promotion resulted in 6,583 orders for 12,026 kits. An email was

## Residential Appliance Rebate Program

### Program Highlights:

BGE’s Residential Appliance Rebate Program achieved 119% of the Q3/Q4 2017 net wholesale
savings forecast, bringing the program to 109% of the total 2017 net wholesale savings forecast.

In Q3/Q4, 5,866 appliances were incented, achieving 101% of the Q3/Q4 measures forecast, and
95% of the total 2017 measures forecast.

- BGE implemented the second and third hybrid water heater Limited Time Offer (LTO) of

2017. The second LTO ran from September 1, 2017 through October 13, 2017, and the
      third LTO began November 19, 2017 and ran until December 31, 2017. During this time
      period, qualifying units purchased from either a participating contractor or a retailer
      received a rebate of $550 - a $50 increase from the regular $500 rebate. For the second
      LTO, 73 units were rebated. Customers and contractors had until November 13, 2017 to
      submit qualifying rebate applications. For the third LTO customers and contractors have
      until January 31, 2018 to submit rebate applications.

- BGE’s participating pool pump contractors submitted 21% more applications on behalf of
  customers during Q3/Q4 2017 than they submitted during Q1/Q2 2017. In the second half
  of 2017, clothes dryers comprised the majority of measures rebated through the program,
  closely followed by tier 3 clothes washers. The following figure shows the breakout of
  appliance measures by type.

- A targeted email campaign was sent to 50,868 customers on November 9, 2017, with the

## Residential Appliance Recycling Program

### Program Highlights:

The Appliance Recycling program achieved 93% of the measure goal and 67% of its net MWh
savings goal for the second half of 2017, bringing the program to 94% of the 2017 measure goal
and 69% of the savings goal for 2017.

[PDF p. 23 | printed p. 10]

In Q3, BGE hosted two Appliance Recycling turn-in events to collect room air conditioners and
dehumidifiers from customers who may not otherwise be able to participate in the program
without a qualifying refrigerator or freezer. The events took place in Columbia and Bel Air
Maryland, and collected 205 room air conditioners and 280 dehumidifiers, for a total of 485 small
units.

In 2017, BGE hosted a total of three turn-in events, collecting 568 room air conditioners and
dehumidifiers. Throughout each event, BGE staff was on hand to cross promote BGE’s
Appliance Rebate and QHEC programs to participating customers.

BGE marketing continued cross promoting the Appliance Recycling Program with the Appliance
Rebate Program in the second half of 2017, which helped boost awareness of the program to
customers who were looking to replace their primary refrigerator or freezer.

The table below shows that refrigerator recycling continues to be the largest category for
appliance recycling.

### Program Changes and Anticipated Changes:

BGE began offering Appliance Recycling customers the opportunity to enroll in the BGE QHEC
program the same day as their unit pick up date. BGE hopes to target customers who are at home
during the day for their appliance collection, adding greater convenience to both programs.

### Marketing:

- A recycling event was held in Bel Air on September 23, 2017 in partnership with the town
  of Bel Air’s community collection day, and collected a total of 264 units. Another event
  was held on September 30, 2017 in partnership with the Columbia Association and

collected a total of 221 units. Both events offered customers a $25 reward for each room
air conditioner or dehumidifier unit dropped off (up to two units per customer).

- New to the event outreach strategy for the fall recycling events, was the inclusion of an

- During the month of November 2017, a bill insert promoting the appliance recycling
  program was included with customer bills beginning Thursday, November 2, 2017 and
  continued through Thursday, November 30, 2017. The bill insert resulted in 174 visits to
  the /Recycle4Rewards vanity URL, with an average session duration of 3:37.

## Residential Quick Home Energy Check-up (QHEC) Program

### Program Highlights:

- The QHEC Program had an outstanding year in 2017 achieving 12,119 MWh net
  wholesale energy savings which represents 115% of the annual net wholesale MWh
  energy savings forecast. The program achieved 107% of the annual participation forecast
  which equates to 36,835 QHEC participants and 462,590 energy efficient measures
  installed. This success represented 122% of the annual measure forecast.

2015-2017 Program cycle goals, as depicted in the following chart:

| Metric | 2017 Percent to Goal | 2015-2017 Cycle Percent to Goal |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Incentives | 94% | 100% |
| Participants | 107% | 103% |
| Measures | 122% | 108% |
| MWh Saved (Net) | 115% | 104% |
| MW Saved (Net) | 125% | 106% |

- Over 95,000 QHECs were delivered during the 2015-2017 program cycle and over
  253,000 since the program began in 2009 achieving an overall 97% customer satisfaction
  rating.
- The Single Family QHEC Program remained consistent throughout 2017, served 25%
  more customers than in any previous year.
- While the Multi-family QHEC Program is no longer the primary source of total QHEC’s
  delivered, there has been a notable uptick in the number of multi-family participants
  served in the second half of 2017 as compared to the first half of 2017, as property
  managers have become more aware of the quality and value offered by ENERGY STAR
  LEDs.

### Program Changes and Anticipated Changes:

- The recent hours-of-use evaluation retroactively decreased lighting savings reported as of
  June 1, 2017 by approximately 17%.
- In Q1 2018, the Program will begin reporting lighting savings using a calculation
  including the actual wattage of removed light bulbs, as opposed to an assumed baseline
  wattage.

[PDF p. 27 | printed p. 14]

### QHEC/PeakRewards Thermostat Pilot:

- In the second half of 2017, the QHEC Program began delivery on a cross-promotional
  pilot with the PeakRewardsSM demand response program. The primary objective of this
  marketing pilot was to determine if customers would be receptive to a simultaneous
  delivery of both the PeakRewardsSM and QHEC programs.
- BGE is linking energy efficiency and demand response with the installation of a smart
  wi-fi thermostat, the ecobee 3 lite, during the QHEC delivery. With one truck roll,
  eligible customers will participate in both the QHEC and PeakRewardsSM Programs.
- Based on the interest expressed in this pilot initiative, beginning in Q1 2018, the QHEC
  Program plans to permanently offer to install the ecobee 3 lite thermostat as an additional
  eligible measure to single family customers who enroll in BGE’s PeakRewardsSM
  program.
- In 2017, this pilot resulted in 139 smart thermostats (PeakRewardsSM participants) being
  installed during a QHEC which produced approximately 27 MWhs of energy savings.

### Marketing:

 The QHEC ‘Quick Way’ campaign reached customers via Broadcast TV through August and
September. Television ads appeared on WBAL, WBFF, WJZ and WMAR and garnered
5,739,881 impressions across 343 spots.
 Two hyper-targeting campaigns in August and September achieved 294,364 impressions for
BGE customers across direct mail postcards, free standing inserts and door hangers.
 Digital campaigns through the second half of 2017 delivered 13,997,269 impressions,
garnering 18,745 clicks, a Click Through Rate (CTR) of 0.13% and 474 conversions across
Google and Bing search, content native, online display banners, Facebook ads, Email, and
online video ads.

## Residential Home Performance with ENERGY STAR® (HPwES) Program

### Program Highlights:

- The Home Performance with ENERGY STAR® (HPwES) Program completed 1,868
  audits, of which 31% converted into jobs in the second half of 2017.
- BGE’s HPwES Program achieved 1054 MWh in Net Wholesale savings in Q3/Q4 2017.
- Performance Based Incentives were successfully implemented in the second half of 2017,
  which increased the average HPwES job savings in the second half by 162% as compared
  to the first half of the year.
- Over 22,500 measures were installed in the second half of the year, with 19,950 installed
  during the initial comprehensive audits and over 2,500 installed during the jobs.

### Program Changes and Anticipated Changes:

- The HPwES Program instituted a significant overhaul in the incentive structure for the
  second half of 2017. Moving from a percentage of cost based incentive structure to a
  performance based incentive (PBI) structure allowed for more comprehensive work-
  scopes by removing the project SIR requirement. As a result of performance based
  incentives, customer job costs and measures increased by 41% and 22%, respectively.

| 2017 Projects | Job Cost (Average) | Measure Quantity (Average) | Customer Incentive (Average) | kWh Savings (Average) |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| All Projects | $4,987 | 9 | $2,124 | 1,444 |
| PBI Projects | $6,188 | 10 | $2,394 | 1,944 |
| Non-PBI Projects | $4,402 | 8 | $1,992 | 1,201 |
| PBI Increase | 41% | 22% | 20% | 62% |

_[source discrepancy: the cover letter (page 10) says the same incentive-structure change increased "customer contract costs and measures" by 131% and 123%; this page and the table above give 41% and 22%.]_

### PBI True Up Evaluation Update:

- Commission Order 88007 approved the Work Group’s request to transition the Home
  Performance with ENERGY STAR (HPwES) program to a performance-based incentive
  structure. The Order specified that the program’s “actual energy savings should be
  tracked in real time, or at a minimum on regular intervals, and should not rely solely on
  year-end data for impact evaluations.” In addition, the Order stated that the utilities must
  “provide quarterly true-up reports and rely on best practices and normalization methods
  when evaluating the modeled versus measured energy savings that result from HPwES
  installations.”

[PDF p. 30 | printed p. 17]

- The evaluation team will analyze 12 months of pre- and 12 months of post-measure utility
  billing data to evaluate energy savings generated by the program. The team will evaluate
  energy savings quarterly using a multifaceted approach that will inform utilities and
  implementers of any issues with ex-ante savings estimates. The team has implemented the
  following enhancements to the core billing analysis to ensure program level ex-ante
  savings estimates are reasonable, identify contractors or projects with inaccurate savings
  estimates, and produce measure-level realization rates (once adequate sample sizes have
  been achieved).[^h1]
  - Work collaboratively with the utilities and program implementers to ensure that
    program tracking data is complete, of high quality, and readily transferrable to the
    evaluation team. Good data quality and transferability are essential to satisfying
    the Commission’s request for faster feedback regarding the performance-based
    incentive structure.
  - Conduct due-diligence reviews of a sample of the program implementers’ energy
    savings estimation models to ensure that project-specific data inputs and
    assumptions are reasonable. The team will conduct these reviews on a quarterly
    basis and will alert the utilities and program implementers immediately of any
    observed anomalies.
  - Use pre-installation billing data to calibrate savings for a sample of participating
    projects to ensure the modeled energy savings estimates are reasonable. The team
    will calibrate savings on a quarterly basis and will alert the utilities and program
    implementers immediately of any observed anomalies.
  - Leverage existing program- and project-level evaluation results to conduct
    additional QC checks on modeled energy savings. The team has compiled a
    repository of thousands of evaluated projects (i.e., homes that participated in the
    EmPOWER HPwES program) with site-specific billing data and can use the data
    to conduct reasonableness checks on modeled energy savings at the project and
    measure levels.
  - Conduct quarterly billing analyses of all participating projects with 12 months of
    post-installation billing data to develop evaluated energy savings and realization
    rates. The team will continue using existing evaluation methods, but on a more
    frequent basis, to develop evaluated results that the utilities and program
    implementers can use to provide quarterly true-up reports to the Commission and
    improve the accuracy of the implementers’ energy savings estimation models.

Independent Evaluator, and the HPwES Work Group. These stakeholders agree that the enhancements will meet the

[^h1]: The team has discussed the evaluation enhancements with the utilities, their HPwES program implementers, the Independent Evaluator, and the HPwES Work Group. These stakeholders agree that the enhancements will meet the reporting requirements specified in Order 88007.

[PDF p. 31 | printed p. 18]

- A key outcome of the evaluation enhancements discussed previously will be finer
  resolution evaluation results, produced on a quarterly basis, that the utilities and their
  program implementers can use to provide quarterly true-up reports to the Commission,
  improve the accuracy of the implementers’ energy savings estimation models, and further
  optimize program performance.[^h2] The team has built effective working relationships with
  utility staff and HPwES program implementers and will leverage these relationships to
  ensure that evaluation findings are efficiently transferred to the utilities and their
  implementers.
- The evaluation team understands the nature of the Commission’s request and the
  importance of providing faster feedback regarding modeled vs. actual energy savings
  estimates during the transition to the performance-based incentive structure. The team is
  committed to providing the utilities, the Commission, and other stakeholders with the
  information needed to support sound decision-making regarding the HPwES program.

The team has implemented the evaluation enhancements discussed in this memo and has
produced preliminary results for discussion with program stakeholders[^h3] to ensure the
stakeholders agree that the enhancements will meet the reporting requirements specified in
Order 88007. The utilities will file the first quarterly true-up with the Commission as part
of the midyear 2018 bi-annual report.

## Residential New Construction Program

## BGE ENERGY STAR® for New Homes Program (New Homes)

### Program Highlights:

BGE’s ENERGY STAR® for New Homes Program achieved 142% of the full-year net wholesale
MWh forecast and 133% of the Cycle-to-Date net wholesale MWh forecast.

- The ENERGY STAR® New Homes Program incentivized 2,567 ENERGY STAR®
  Certified homes in 2017. The program introduced smart thermostat incentives in Q2 of
  2017 and has seen swift adoption since its introduction. The program has incentivized
  over 17,400 homes since its inception.

The program was also presented with the EPA’s ENERGY STAR® Certified New Homes

-

Market Leader Award. The award recognizes the BGE new Homes Program’s help in
promoting energy efficient construction and incentivizing close to 3,000 homes in 2016
alone. BGE has been an ENERGY STAR® Partner since 2009.

- The BGE New Homes Program continued to focus on training during the second half of

2017. The program hosted the Ekotrope training in November. Ekotrope is the new rater
      training software tool offering full capability for HERS, ENERGY STAR®, code
      compliance and utility rebates.

- The program provided monthly technical bulletins and increased the selection of online
  learning courses to address commonly found mistakes observed in the field. Topics
  addressed in the second half of the year included: Modeling Advanced Framing
  Techniques, Modeling Windows and Walls in REM Rate and the smart thermostat rebate
  submission process.
- BGE’s ENERGY STAR® New Homes Program launched an updated online home
  registration and rebate platform in March of 2017. This online system helps to streamline
  the rebate application process. It enables builders and raters to register and submit rebate
  applications in bulk and cut down on the administrative time required for each application.
- Of the 2,567 certified homes that have been incentivized by the BGE New Homes
  Program, 96 were multi-family low rise units. Multi-family low rise building designs are
  often influenced by other key stakeholders like architects. In an attempt to reach these
  decision makers, the BGE New Homes Program hosted a lunch and learn presentation
  with the American Institute of Architects’ Committee on the Environment and Resiliency
  and Allied Members Committee.

[PDF p. 35 | printed p. 22]

### Program Changes and Anticipated Changes:

For the 2018-2020 program cycle, the BGE ENERGY STAR® New Homes Program will maintain its existing program offerings and include two additional enhancements:

1. The Residential New Construction Concierge
2. Smart Thermostat Measure

[PDF p. 36 | printed p. 23]

Consumers that purchase ENERGY STAR® Certified Homes are living in homes that are much
different from homes built even five years ago. An ENERGY STAR® Certified home, has
automated ventilation, right-sized air conditioners, tighter construction, better filtration, and
consistent temperatures throughout the entire house. Learning about how to live efficiently and
comfortably in these homes will take training and special customer service. The New Homes
Concierge will provide BGE customers insight on how to live efficiently in their homes by
meeting with new homeowners -- ENERGY STAR® participating and non-ENERGY STAR® --
to discuss ways to achieve additional savings in their homes. The discussions will emphasize
energy efficiency benefits they may already have purchased in the home, while identifying new
opportunities through a walk-thru audit and installation of additional energy savings measures,
such as LEDs and a smart energy device applicable to their needs. The New Homes Concierge
will accelerate and enhance the current BGE EmPOWER Maryland plans by increasing
participation in existing programs where there is a continued opportunity for savings.

In response to consumers’ increasing interest in smart homes, we continued the smart thermostat
add-on incentive. As smart thermostats are being finalized for the 2018 Mid-Atlantic TRM, we
anticipate a savings range from 300-500 kWh per home depending on size and fuel type. Based
on a conservative estimate using this range, smart thermostats are a cost effective measure that
can deliver meaningful energy savings. Participating builders will receive an incentive for
installing qualifying smart thermostats. BGE customers who purchase a participating home with
a smart thermostat may also be recruited to participate in the current PeakRewards program.

### Marketing:

- The New Homes digital media campaign continued in the second half of the year with
  Bing and Google search ads.
- A flyer and door hanger were created for subcontractor to promote the concierge services
  available to customers who purchased an ENERGY STAR® certified new home through a
  participating builder.
- To promote the concierge services, a targeted email campaign was sent via Eloqua to
  2,791 customers who have purchased an ENERGY STAR® certified new home through
  our participating builders on November 1, 2017 with the subject line “Learn more about
  your energy-efficient new home”. It was opened 996 times with an open rate of 22.51%
  and a click through rate of 0.54%. A second targeted email was sent to 2,152 customers
  who have purchased an ENERGY STAR® certified new home through our participating
  builders on December 5, 2017 with the subject line “Learn more about your energy-
  efficient new home”. It was opened 292 times with an open rate of 13.59% and a click
  through rate of 0.47%.

[PDF p. 37 | printed p. 24]

## Residential HVAC Program

### Program Highlights:

- BGE’s Residential HVAC Program reported over 7,300 ENERGY STAR® qualified units
  installed during the second half of 2017, saving over 5,600 Gross Wholesale MWh.
- The program’s contractor participation increased 11% in 2017 as compared to the 2016
  participation year. Of the participating contractors in 2016 and 2017, 41% reported an
  increase in energy efficient equipment installations.
- In closing out the 2015-2017 Program Cycle, BGE’s Residential HVAC program incented
  almost 38,000 qualified units, achieving over 26,000 Gross Wholesale MWh in savings,
  almost 97% and 100% of the overall program goals, respectively.

### Marketing:

- A targeted email campaign was sent to 5,057 customers on July 20, 2017, with the subject
  line “Here’s a cool way to save this summer – Rebates on AC equipment”. It was opened
  1,383 times with an open rate of 22.76%.
- The fall heating digital media campaign launched on October 1, 2017 and ran through
  November 30, 2017 with Bing and Google text ads and display retargeting ads, Google
  Display ads, online banner ads, online video, and Facebook ads.
- A targeted email campaign was sent to 27,000 customers on October 26, 2017, with the
  subject line “Increase your comfort and save this fall with rebates on heating equipment”.

It was opened 6,889 times with a unique open rate of 17.97% and a click through rate of
0.31%. The original email was redeployed on November 2, 2017 to 4,173 customers with

[PDF p. 38 | printed p. 25]

the subject line “Don’t miss your chance for fall savings on heating equipment”. The
second email was opened 3,237 times with a unique open rate of 57.42% and a click
through rate of 0.69%.

## Commercial & Industrial (C&I) Summary

- During the second half of 2017, the C&I portfolio continued to see a strong growth trend.

The C&I programs yielded over 108,000 MWh compared to its forecast of 93,000 MWh
achieving 117% of its second half forecast.

- The total energy savings for all of the C&I programs for 2017 was over 194,000 MWh
  achieving 101% of the 2017 forecast.
- The Small Business Energy Solutions program did not achieve its annual forecast (69%)
  primarily because interior TLED’s were not available in the program. As this TLED
  technology was approved for the 2018-2020 program cycle, BGE believes that it will see a
  significant upswing in 2018.
- During the second half of 2017, the Energy Solutions for Business (ESfB) Program
  continued to be a strong contributor towards BGE’s C&I portfolio. In the second half,
  over 1,000 ESfB Program projects (Prescriptive and Custom) were completed,
  representing over 57,000 MWh in annualized energy savings or 145% of its Q3/Q4 energy
  savings forecast. The total energy savings forecast for 2017 was over 92,000 MWh or
  119% of its annual energy savings forecast.
- BGE had two CHP projects which went online and started generating savings in 2017.

The 2 MW projects at Baltimore Washington Medical Center and the University of
Maryland St. Joseph’s Medical Center provided over 25,000 MWh.

- BGE’s Commercial Midstream Lighting (Instant Lighting Discounts) Program achieved
  over 17,000 MWh for the second half bringing the total 2017 savings performance to over
  45,000 MWh and achieving 158% of its 2017 forecast.
- The Building Tune-up and Building Operators Training programs greatly accelerated in
  the second half of the year to produce over 10,000 MWh of 428% of its second half
  forecast. Together, the programs delivered over 13,000 MWh or 218% of the annual
  energy saving forecast.
- The Master-Metered Multi-Family QHEC program was delivered to over 1,000
  participants in 2017 which achieved 52% of the annual forecast. The program provided
  over 280 MWh achieving 33% of its annual energy savings forecast.
- BGE’s Small Business, Midstream Lighting and Energy Solutions for Business all
  underwent a comprehensive “Customer Journey Mapping” process in 2017 which
  produced numerous suggestions for process improvements for the upcoming 2018-2020
  program cycle.

## Small Business Energy Solutions Program

### Program Highlights:

- The Small Business Energy Solutions achieved 9,000 MWh in energy savings in the
  second half of 2017. Although there were gains in the second half the program, BGE was
  unable to meet its energy savings forecast for 2017 achieving 69% of the annual forecast.

[PDF p. 41 | printed p. 28]

- The primary factor contributing to the shortfall was the fact that interior Tubular LED’s
  (TLED’s) florescent lamp replacements were not offered in the program. As the lighting
  market has evolved, BGE found that most prospective customers are no longer interested
  in the older florescent lighting technologies. BGE will be offering TLED’s in the 2018-20
  program cycle to alleviate this situation.
- For 2017, LED outdoor lighting was a key area for the SBES program. Not only is
  outdoor lighting a very effective way to reduce energy consumption, but it also provides a
  safer, more inviting environment.
- BGE believes the Small Business Energy Advance (SBEA) program continues to be an
  important driver for the SBES program because it allows cash-strapped businesses to take
  advantage of opportunities provided by the program.
- For 2017, the SBEA provided 286 advances totaling approximately $715,000. SBEA
participation was 44% of eligible SBES projects ($1,000 minimum customer cost). The
  current payback rate approaches 90% which has enabled the original funds to be
  “recycled” extending the life of the fund.

### Program Changes and Anticipated Changes:

- Please refer to BGE’s 2018 – 2020 EmPOWER Maryland filing for program changes.

  programs.

## Partnering with Service Providers:

- BGE held seven Service Provider trainings for its Energy Solutions for Business Program,
  resulting in a combined attendance of 116 attendees from 83 unique companies during
  Q3/Q4 of 2017. Throughout 2017, BGE held 20 training sessions that resulted in a total
  combined attendance of 319 unique individuals from 190 unique companies for the year.
- A variety of new training initiatives were introduced in 2017 to assist in better training
  Service Providers. BGE began tailoring designated sessions to “new” Service Providers
  who are brand new to EmPOWER Maryland programs and BGE’s Energy Solutions for
  Business Program to provide an introduction to the portfolio and review essential concepts
  at a measured pace.
- Since 2016, business/trade licensing and insurance requirements were introduced amongst
  Service Providers in an effort to increase the level of quality control from Service
  Providers who were participating in the program and servicing BGE customers. BGE is
  pleased to see these requirements have not negatively impacted participation.
- During the second half of the year, 86 new companies applied and were approved as
  Service Providers for the Energy Solutions for Business program, bringing the annual total
  of newly approved Service Providers in 2017 to 193. The total number of approved
  Service Providers at the end of 2017 was 330, demonstrating that interest and willingness
  to participate remains as strong as ever.

## C&I Building Tune-Up & Operators Training Program

### Program Highlights:

- The Building Tune-Up program delivered over 8,000 MWh or 218% of its annual energy
  savings forecast while the Building Operator Training Program delivered over 4,000
  MWh in energy savings and achieved 219% of its 2017 annual energy savings forecast.
- In 2017, Enhanced Unitary (Rooftop Unit) Tune-ups have become one of the leading
  drivers in the program. This previously underappreciated tune-up procedure gives the
  program access to tens of thousands of standalone unitary rooftop units within the service
  territory.
- BGE’s innovative use of Building Analytics to enhance customer adoption of Building
  Tune-up was recognized as Analytics Project of the Year at the CS Week conference in
  May of 2017 and awarded 2016 Project of the Year by Energy Manager Today magazine.

## Combined Heat & Power (CHP) Program

### Program Highlights:

- In the second half, BGE successfully launched its second CHP project in 2017 for the
  University of Maryland St. Joseph’s Medical Center. This is similar to the Baltimore
  Washington Medical Center unit that was installed in the first half that also involved a 2
  MW project. The project received a $350,000 installation incentive through the Program.

This unit is using the thermal heat to preheat boilers, and provide steam heating, domestic
hot water heating, and space heating.

- The 2017 CHP projects are the second and third 2 MW systems to be installed in a
  hospital setting. BGE is using these successes to demonstrate how CHP systems can work
  for other hospitals in the BGE territory.
- Additionally, BGE focused on implementing the ongoing marketing strategy and outreach
  to several potential CHP candidates in the BGE service territory. A direct result of this
  effort has generated a strong interest from at least three local hospitals. Hospitals are very
  good candidates for CHP units which improve capability and could allow them to run in
  “island mode” if there is a major interruption to the power grid.
- In the Fall of 2017, a personalized email was sent to targeted customers to advise them of
  the additional grant funding available by the State of Maryland for fiscal year 2018 CHP
  project. Several customers responded and were excited to hear about this opportunity and
  how it could impact their progress with CHP installations.

## C&I Overall Marketing Outreach

To continue to increase participation levels and achieve its energy savings forecast, the BGE
Smart Energy Savers Program marketing approach has continued to evolve, incorporating insights
gained from actual market response to the programs and by analyzing website trends and
advertising response rates. The integrated marketing campaign, using a sector-based approach,
reaches a variety of businesses and educates them about potential savings opportunities specific to
their industries. BGE marketing efforts achieved a notable 118% increase in visits to the business
web pages, compared to web traffic during the same time period in 2016.

### Collateral:

- Testimonials from customers representing a broad range of industries help customers
  envision their own successes and provides a path to making similar energy efficiency
  changes within their organizations. BGE refreshed the Energy Solutions for Business
  brochure in June 2017 to spotlight the Columbia Association and National Aquarium
  testimonials including portraits, quotes and savings numbers.

[PDF p. 50 | printed p. 37]

### Lighting Tip Sheets:

- Lighting tip sheets, developed in collaboration with account managers directly
  interfacing with customers and contractors on a day-to-day basis, are used to effectively
  communicate the specific, energy-efficient lighting options available to businesses. In
  2017, the Exterior LED Lighting, Fluorescent Troffer Alternatives, Designing with Light
  and Advanced Lighting Controls tip sheets were updated to reflect new lighting incentive
  levels.

### Press Event:

- Part of BGE’s marketing strategy is to highlight program champions and their unique
  projects that have received incentives through the program. In June 2017, BGE presented
  Harvest Fare Supermarket with a big check for the energy efficient improvements and
  upgrades made through the program. The Baltimore Sun and The Aegis published articles
  covering this presentation.

[PDF p. 51 | printed p. 38]

### Customer Engagement:

- BGE sponsored the Baltimore Business Journal Real Estate event on April 27, 2017. This
  year the real estate event took on a new format focusing on networking and highlighting
  one real estate award winner for Best in Show. A half-page color ad was included with
  the sponsorship. Over 150 commercial real estate and business executives attended the
  event.

### Lighting Technology Seminar:

- On June 8, 2017, BGE along with Pepco and Delmarva Power hosted the 2017 Lighting
  Technology Seminar at the Turf Valley Resort in Ellicott City. This event had over 230
  attendees for the seminar and product showcase. The event featured industry expert
  speakers, program participant speakers, and a panel discussion. The event also included a
  Lighting Product Showcase with 17 vendors displaying LED products and BGE had a
  display promoting the BGE SESP.

- A variety of marketing tactics drive greater awareness of and participation in all C&I
  programs. In 2017, online and offline advertising and promotion for C&I programs has

  customer spotlights ran throughout the period in various publications such as The
  Baltimore Business Journal and I-95 Business Magazine. Digital ads ran on Google and

  Business Journal.

## Behavioral and Demand Response Programs

## PeakRewards℠

### Program Highlights:

The PeakRewards℠ Air Conditioner Program ended the second half of the year with demand
response devices in 33% of eligible homes (i.e. those with central air conditioning) and 351 MW
of peak load reduction capability.

The PeakRewards℠ Water Heater Programs peak load reduction capability stood at 6 MW at the
end of 2017.

As of the end of 2017:

- There were more than 312,000 Air Conditioning and 22,000 Water Heater customers
  receiving credits.
- Over 345,000 air conditioner devices and over 22,000 water heater devices were
  participating in PeakRewards℠, for a total of over 367,000 total devices participating.
- New Customer Acquisition Installs: There were 3,796 new air conditioner and 308 new
  water heater devices installed, for a total of 4,104 devices installed in Q3 and Q4.
  - For the full year, we saw 6,109 new air conditioner and 1,009 new water heater
    devices installed, for a total of 7,118 devices.
- There were no water heater cycling events.
- There were no market or reliability-driven cycling events.
- A PeakRewards℠ in-season test event was held on September 19, 2017 to perform
  operational testing of the systems. The test event included all cycling levels and all event
  types (emergency and non-emergency with transition in and out of the events). All
  procedures and customer communications were tested. Customer feedback via the call
  centers and social media was minimal.

| Event Date | Event Type | Event Hours |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Tuesday, September 19, 2017 | In-season test event | 11:30am – 2:30pm |

[PDF p. 56 | printed p. 43]

## Lag Times Between Enrollment and Installation

- An average of 24 technicians and 2 operability technicians were working through Q3 2017
  and an average of 26 technicians and 1 operability technician were working through Q4

2017.

    - At the end of Q4 2017, 26 technicians were working on the PeakRewards℠

program.

- Average number of days to complete an order:
  - Q3 2017 was 14 days for thermostats, 23 days for A/C switches, and 22 days for
    water heater switches.
  - Q4 2017 was 13 days for thermostats, 13 days for A/C switches, and 20 days for
    water heater switches.
  - The average days to complete a new smart thermostat was 17 days over Q3 and
    Q4.
- There were 7,606 smart thermostats installed in Q3 and Q4 2017 comprised of both new
  customer acquisitions and Upro device upgrades.
- There were 714 PeakRewards® customers awaiting installation as of December 31, 2017.

This included 520 smart thermostats, 91 water heater switches, 83 Upro thermostats, 20
A/C switches.

## Other Programs

## Conservation Voltage Reduction (CVR)

BGE completed its CVR pilot in 2014 involving fourteen feeders and BGE started full scale
deployment, namely the installation of physical equipment in the field, as well as the installation
of the head-end CVR system. The head-end system went live in Q1 2015.

To accurately calculate the savings rate for this program, one full year experience is necessary to
compare against the pre-deployment baseline. Accordingly, current period savings are calculated
based on the full number of deployments completed through the end of 2017 using the savings
rate based on one full year experience on the actual deployments through the end of 2016. Any
adjustments realized based on the continuing one full year experience of deployments will be
trued up in subsequent reports in the accompanying Program Summary Tables.

## BGE Smart Energy Rewards® (SER) Program

- 2017 was the fifth summer season for the BGE Smart Energy Rewards® (SER) Program.

The program called two Energy Savings Days (ESDs): July 13, 2017 and July 21, 2017.

- Program participation stayed consistent with prior years. Participation is defined as
  customers who earned a bill credit during an ESD. We have included the below charts for
  reference:

| 2017 Event Data | 7/13/2017 | 7/21/2017 |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Max Heat Index | 103 | 100 |
| Average Event Heat Index | 101 | 97 |
| Participation Rate | 70% | 78% |

| 2016 Event Data | 7/8/2016 | 7/14/2016 | 7/25/2016 |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| Max Heat Index | 96 | 100 | 108 |
| Average Event Heat Index | 92 | 99 | 107 |
| Participation Rate | 80% | 66% | 66% |

- Over the two ESDs, BGE sent more than 3.8 million messages to customers by phone,
  email or text notifying them of the event.
- By the end of the 2017 season, more than 1,095,000 customers were eligible to participate
  in SER.
- The load reducing capability of the SER program experienced in 2017 is estimated to be
  330 MW.

[PDF p. 61 | printed p. 48]

### 2017 Program Highlights:

- Two Energy Savings Days on July 13, 2017 and July 21, 2017
- Over 1,095,000 residential customers eligible
- 74% average participation
- $6.13 average credit for participating customers
- $6.1 million in bill credits in total paid to participating customers for the two events

### Program Changes and Anticipated Changes:

### Marketing:

During the two Energy Savings Days in 2017, messages were featured on BGE.com, the upfront
message at the BGE call center, BGE’s social media pages (Facebook/Twitter), included in a
press release and through our TV network promotional partners (ABC, NBC and CBS local
affiliates). Additionally, BGE partnered with the Baltimore Orioles to feature ads within the
stadium the day before an Energy Savings Day and at games during the summer for greater
visibility among BGE customers.

- An email went out to all customers that participate in Energy Savings Days and the
  PeakRewards program to recap how the summer programs went, the credits customers
  earned and cross-promote other programs and services from BGE that can help customers
  save energy and money. In total, three emails were sent to 498,008 customers between the
  two programs. The emails had an average open rate of 36% (197,626 total opens).

## BGE’s Smart Energy Manager® (SEM) Program

### 2017 Program Highlights:

BGE’s Smart Energy Manager® (SEM) program uses a variety of channels (web, email, text,
automated phone dialer, and paper reports) to help customers understand their energy usage and
thereby encourage energy efficiency and conservation. Customers become eligible as their smart
meters are installed and become ready for billing.

Program results include:

- Over 920,000 customers enrolled in SEM as of December 2017 slightly up from 904,000
  in June 2017 and 901,000 at the end of 2016.
- Over 14.8 million Home Energy Reports sent to customers through December 2017 up
  from 12.2 million in June 2017 and 10.3 million through December 2016.
- 559,312 unique customer visits to the web-based energy management tool spending an
  average of 2 minutes and 40 seconds for each visit.
- 136,998 MWh reduced in 2017, up from a 126,693 reduction in 2016. BGE reports
  savings with deemed savings from other Energy Efficiency programs backed out. 1,443
  MWh are deemed to be from other Energy Efficiency programs, backing those out brings
  the adjusted 2017 total to 135,555 MWh.
- At a premise level, customers reduced gas usage by 2.56 million therms through the
  program. This represents an increase from 2016 when customers reduced gas usage by
  nearly 1.93 million therms.

### Program Changes and Anticipated Changes:

- Launched the Net Metering online widget which allows Net Metering customers to view
  their net energy credit balances from month to month.
- Launched a dual fuel version of the Weekly Usage Report which provides dual fuel (gas
  and electric) customers a comparison of both their gas and electric usage week over week.

This product had previously only been available for one fuel type (electric). This
enhancement provides customers with more insights for their “total bill” versus insights
that were previously specific to individual fuel types.

- For planned enhancement work related to the Smart Energy Manager Program, please
  refer to BGE’s 2018 – 2020 EmPOWER Maryland filing.

[PDF p. 64 | printed p. 51]

### Marketing:

- During the fall and winter months, emails are sent to all customers with an email address
  and access to My Account. These emails include three articles that inform customers of
  the various ways My Account and the online energy management tools can help them save
  energy and money as well as provide season energy efficiency tips. In 2017, these emails
  went out in the months of January-April 2017 and November-December 2017. The
  average email list contained approximately 415,000 emails and the email had a 53.5%
  open rate, which is well above the industry average. The click through rate of these
  emails was 3.8%.
- This year, six videos were created to provide a short overview of specific My Account
  tools or benefits provided by smart meters. Each video, 60 seconds or less, was featured
  either in one of the My Account monthly emails, as part of a social media
  (Facebook/Instagram) advertising campaign or is now featured on BGE.com/EnergyUse
  to help customers better understand the uses and benefits of an online account. A social
  media campaign promoting the Home Energy Report video began running on November
  30, 2017 and has reached 38,000 people with 74,000 impressions so far.

[PDF p. 65 | printed p. 52]

- A letter and brochure were created and mailed to approximately 207,000 customers in
  October 2017 that were identified as not having an online account but were very likely to
  create one if they understood the benefits. The brochure included an overview of specific
  My Account tools and cross promoted other BGE programs that can help save customers
  energy and money. The letter included a customized URL for each person that received
  the letter so they could go online and create their own account. Of the customers that
  received the letter, 1,582 used the personalized URL in the letter to create their online
  account.

[PDF p. 66 | printed p. 53]

calculation of reported energy and demand savings were implemented effective June 1 and
applied retroactively to June 1, 2015. The impact on Gross Wholesale energy and demand
savings from these adjustments is a follows:

| **Demand Savings (MW)** |  |  |  |
| Residential | 57.525 | 62.528 | 5.003 |
| Commercial | 79.835 | 74.379 | (5.456) |
| Total (no label printed) | 137.360 | 136.907 | (0.454) |
