1. State which categories are calculated based upon the Commission approved filing. If
   calculations are not based on the approved filing, identify the assumptions used.

Categories are calculated based upon the Commission approvals, included on the original
and subsequent program filings and General Awareness allocation.

2. Define what constitutes a participant and measure
   Following is a summary of Participants and Measures for BGE’s Energy Efficiency
   Programs:

Residential Energy Efficiency Programs

## Residential Lighting Program

Participant: A household that purchases discounted screw-in CFL or LED bulbs or fixtures
from a participating retailer or who uses a coupon to purchase screw-in CFLs.

This number is derived from the number of bulbs sold using the bulbs per
household factor of 24 bulbs per household as used in BGE’s August 12, 2012
filing.

Measure: CFLs and LEDs of various wattages

## Residential Appliance Rebate Program

Participant: A residential customer who purchases a qualified appliance and submits a
rebate application form. Note – if a customer purchases an appliance and
submits a rebate application, that customer is counted as one participant. If
that rebate application is for multiple appliances, that customer is still counted
as a single participant. However, if later that same customer purchases another
appliance and submits a new rebate application, they will be counted as another
participant.

Measures: Qualified appliances under the program - Clothes Washer, Refrigerator, Room
A/C, Water Heater, Freezer, Dehumidifier.

## Residential Appliance Recycling Program

Participant: Residential Customer who requests removal of a qualified refrigerator, freezer
or room A/C from home (the latter if already removing a refrigerator or
freezer). Note – if a customer recycles an appliance and receives an incentive,
that customer is counted as one participant. If that incentive is for multiple
appliances, that customer is still counted as a single participant. However, if
later that same customer recycles another appliance and receives an additional
rebate, they will be counted as another participant.

Measure: Removal of refrigerator and/or freezer, and/or Room A/C

[PDF p. 121 | printed p. 66]

## Residential Quick Home Energy Check-Up (QHEC) Program

Participant: A residential customer who has a walk-through of home by a check-up
professional and accepts installation of at least 3 energy savings measures as
recommended by professional.

Measure: CFLs of various wattages, low-flow showerheads, pipe insulation, faucet
aerators, water heater tank wrap, water heater temperature turndown, smart
strips

## Residential Home Performance with ENERGY STAR® (HPwES) Program

Participant: A residential customer who receives a home energy audit by a Home
Performance Contractor is counted under number of completed audits in the
mini-table. A residential customer who installs energy efficiency
improvements recommended by the above contractor is counted under
completed projects when the job is finished.

Measure: Insulation, Duct Sealing, Air Sealing, CFL Installation, HVAC replacements or
tune-ups, low-flow showerheads, faucet aerators, appliance replacements, and
other energy efficient installations.

## Residential New Construction Program

### Single Family Housing:

Participant: A builder or home purchaser that contracts with ENERGY STAR builder to
construct a new home that meets ENERGY STAR New Home Qualification.

Measure: New Home, 2-on-2 condos, townhouses (Tiers 2, 3, or 4)

### Multifamily Low Rise:

Participant: A builder, developer or architect that contracts with ENERGY STAR builder
to construct multifamily buildings with three stories or fewer above grade, or
four or five stories above grade that have been permitted residential or with
residential use group, have their own heating, cooling and hot water systems
separate from other units and where dwelling units occupy 80% or more of the
available square footage of the building that meets the EPA ENERGY STAR
Multifamily New Construction Program Decision Tree.

Measure: New Multifamily low rise unit (Tier 1)

## Residential HVAC Program

Participant: A residential customer who purchases new HVAC equipment, completes duct
sealing or has a diagnostic tune-up performed.

Measure: New Furnace, Heat Pump, Central or Room A/C; duct sealing of system;
diagnostic tune up of HVAC equipment; proper sizing, check-up, and install of
HVAC equipment

[PDF p. 122 | printed p. 67]

Residential Gas Conversion (BGE Natural Gas Connection) Program -- (Discontinued as of June
30, 2016)

Participant: A residential customer who installs gas service, and purchases new gas HVAC
equipment and/or purchases new gas water heating equipment
Measure: New gas furnace and/or new gas water heater while removing the existing
electric equipment. Note – Central ACs and Heat Pumps are permissible
measures if the customer installs them when the gas furnace or gas water heater
is installed (and existing electric equipment replaced).

Commercial & Industrial (C&I) Energy Efficiency Programs

## Small Business Energy Solutions Program

Participant: A small business customer who has qualified lighting, refrigeration, vending
machine/occupancy control and/or DHW measures installed
Measure: Lighting replacements, refrigeration, vending machine, occupancy sensor or
DHW measures installed
Energy Solutions for Business (Prescriptive) Program
Participant: An I&C customer that installs any number of qualified energy efficient
measures.

Measure: Lighting replacements or new installations, unitary HVAC equipment, chillers,
VFDs, refrigeration, kitchen equipment, vending machine controls
Energy Solutions for Business (Custom) Program
Participant: An C&I customer that contracts to complete energy efficient work not covered
under the prescriptive program and provides documents that can be reviewed
and prove the energy savings.

Measure: The total project counts as one measure
New Construction (now a component of the Custom Program)

Participant: An I&C customer that is completing a new construction or major renovation
project and is interested in pursuing the Comprehensive Design or Green
Building Track.

Measure: The total project counts as one measure

## C&I Building Tune-Up (Retrocommissioning) Program

Participant: A C&I customer that works with an RCx Service Provider to enhance their
O&M procedures.

[PDF p. 123 | printed p. 68]

Measure: Each project counts as one measure
C&I Building Operator Training Program (NEW)

Participant: A C&I customer who completes courses and/or certifications related to
reducing electrical usage or otherwise enhancing the energy efficiency of their
facility.

Measure: Each course counts as a measure

## Combined Heat & Power (CHP) Program

Participant: A C&I customer that is installing a combined heat and power system for their
facility where the waste heat is utilized to offset other energy sources and all
generation is used on site.

Measure: The total project counts as one measure
C&I Midstream Lighting Program (NEW)

Participant: A C&I customer that installs any number of eligible lamps purchased through a
program distributor.

Measure: Number of eligible lighting equipment.

Master-Metered Multi-Family Quick Home Energy Check-Up
Participant: A BGE customer previously unable to participate in the residential QHEC
program because they are not residentially metered. Customers living in a
master-metered residence who are ineligible to participate in the Department of
Housing and Community Develop (DHCD) and Multi-family Energy
Efficiency and Housing Affordability (MEEHA) Program will be served by
this Master-Metered QHEC Program offering.

The count of units that is managed/owned by a property manager/owner who
agrees to a walk-through by a check-up professional and accepts installation of
at least 3 energy savings measures as agreed upon by the professional and the
manager/owner of the entire property.

Measure: CFLs of various wattages, an LED, low-flow showerheads, pipe insulation,
faucet aerators, water heater tank wrap, water heater temperature turndown,
smart strips
3. Factors used to calculate energy savings and demand reduction
Following is a summary of the factors that are used to calculate energy and demand
savings for BGE’s Energy Efficiency Programs:

[PDF p. 124 | printed p. 69]

Residential Energy Efficiency Programs

## Residential Lighting Program

Energy Deemed Savings (Base Wattage, Change Wattage, and Hours of Use)
calculated based on the Mid-Atlantic TRM where applicable
Demand Deemed Savings (Annual kWh Savings, Coincidence Factor) calculated based
on the Mid-Atlantic TRM where applicable

## Residential Appliances Rebate Program

Energy Deemed Savings calculated based on the Mid-Atlantic TRM where applicable
Demand Annual savings, coincidence factor calculated based on the Mid-Atlantic TRM
where applicable

## Residential Appliances Recycling Program

Energy Deemed Savings calculated based on the Mid-Atlantic TRM where applicable
Demand Annual savings, coincidence factor calculated based on the Mid-Atlantic TRM
where applicable

## Residential Quick Home Energy Check-Up (QHEC) Program

Energy Deemed Savings calculated based on the Mid-Atlantic TRM where applicable
Demand Annual savings, coincidence factor calculated based on the Mid-Atlantic TRM
where applicable

## Residential Home Performance with ENERGY STAR® (HPwES) Program

Energy Existing or code energy use, change energy use, hours of operation
Demand Annual savings, coincidence factor

## Residential New Construction Program

Single Family Housing
Energy Code measure kWh, change measure kWh, hours of use
Demand Annual savings, coincidence factor

### Multifamily / Condominium

Energy: Code measure kWh, change measure kWh, hours of use
Demand: Annual savings, coincidence factor

### Limited Income

Energy Existing measure energy use, change measure energy use, hours of use
Demand Annual savings, coincidence factor

## Residential HVAC Program

[PDF p. 125 | printed p. 70]

Energy Existing or Code measure Energy Use, Change measure Energy Use, hours of
use calculated based on the Mid-Atlantic TRM where applicable
Demand Annual savings, coincidence factor calculated based on the Mid-Atlantic TRM
where applicable
Residential Gas Conversion (BGE Natural Gas Connection) Program -- (Discontinued as of June
30, 2016)

Energy: Existing or Code measure Energy Use, Change measure Energy Use, hours of
use calculated based on the Mid-Atlantic TRM where applicable
Demand: Annual savings, coincidence factor calculated based on the Mid-Atlantic TRM
where applicable
Commercial & Industrial (C&I) Energy Efficiency Programs

## Small Business Energy Solutions Program

Energy Existing kWh, change kWh, hours of use, hours of reduction due to controls
Demand Annual savings, coincidence factor
Energy Solutions for Business (Prescriptive) Program
Energy Existing or code energy use, change energy use, building type, specific
program information, hours of operation, hours of reduction due to controls
Demand Annual savings, coincidence factor
Energy Solutions for Business (Custom) Program
Energy Existing or code measure energy use, change energy use, custom specific
information
Demand Annual savings, coincidence factor
New Construction (component of Custom Program)

Energy Existing or code measure energy use, change energy use, Leadership in Energy
and Environment Design (LEED) Green Building Rating System
Demand Annual savings, coincidence factor

## C&I Building Tune-Up (Retrocommissioning) Program

Energy Past energy consumption, modeled energy consumption
Demand Annual savings, coincidence factor
C&I Building Operator Training Program (NEW)

Energy: Calculated per class or certification
Demand: Annual savings, coincidence factor

[PDF p. 126 | printed p. 71]

## Combined Heat & Power (CHP) Program

Energy Actual generation usage provided by required customer sub-metering
installation
Demand Annual savings, coincidence factor
C&I Midstream Lighting Program (NEW)

Energy: Deemed Savings calculated based on the Mid-Atlantic TRM where applicable,
building type, and projected hours of usage as determined by Mid-Atlantic
TRM
Demand: Annual savings, coincidence factor
Master-Metered Multi-Family Quick Home Energy Check-Up Program (NEW)

Energy: Deemed Savings calculated based on the Mid-Atlantic TRM where applicable
Demand: Annual savings, coincidence factor calculated based on the Mid-Atlantic TRM
where applicable
4. Calculation used to determine Lifecycle Savings with an example
Project Lifecycle Savings = Annual Savings X Measure Life (in years)

Example, for a residential appliance rebate – Pool Pump Variable Speed
Lifecycle Savings are calculated as 594 kWh (measure annualized savings) X 10 (years) =
5,940 kWh
5. Avoided costs assumptions used for energy and demand calculations. Update when these
changes occur.

Refer to Maryland Energy Administration’s cost effectiveness studies for 2015-2017
EmPOWER Maryland filings for avoided costs assumptions.

6. Line loss factors
   Assumed losses being used to provide data on actual energy and demand are the same as
   those used by BGE, for example, when reporting to PJM and are as follows:

|  | Average Annual - MWh | Summer Peak - MW |
| --- | --- | --- |
| 34 kV | 2.47% | 3.50% |
| 13 and 4 kV | 3.66% | 5.40% |
| Secondary Voltage | 6.67% | 8.90% |

[PDF p. 127 | printed p. 72]

7. Note how Forecasted Total Program Expenditures and Participant Forecasted based upon
   the Commission approved filing (i.e., divide by 4 then by 3 or was it based upon season
   trend predictions)

Forecasted Total Program Expenditures and Participants are based upon the Commission
approved filing, based upon seasonal trend predictions.

8. Note start date for annualized saving calculations
   January 1 of the relevant year
9. Fast Track Inclusions are noted on the relevant templates. The program dates included are
   January 2008 through April 2009.

10. Include any additional assumption used to comprise the filing.

For PeakRewardsSM, only active enrollments/devices are reported (those set to other than
0% cycling).
